Introduction\n23:### 1.1 Terminology\n27:### 1.2 Transport\n31:## 2. Connection Establishment\n33:### 2.1 Endpoint\n50:### 2.2 Authentication\n78:### 2.3 Session Initialization\n99:## 3. Message Format\n112:## 4. Client-to-Server Messages\n114:### 4.1 user_message\n134:- `content` MUST NOT be empty or whitespace-only after trimming.\n135:- `content` MUST NOT exceed 10,000 characters.\n136:- Violation of these constraints results in an `error` response with code `INVALID_MESSAGE`.\n147:### 4.2 ping\n163:## 5. Server-to-Client Messages\n165:### 5.1 connected\n179:### 5.2 entry_upsert\n203:- If `index` equals the current array length, the entry is appended.\n204:- If `index` is within the existing array bounds, the entry at that position is replaced.\n205:- The client MUST NOT assume entries arrive in sequential index order during tool loops.\n206:- A repeat `entry_upsert` at an index the client already holds, carrying an assistant entry with empty `content`, is a **retraction**: the server is withdrawing text it already sent. See Section 5.5.\n208:### 5.3 entry_delta\n228:- The client MUST concatenate `delta` to `entry.content` at the given index.\n229:- The referenced entry MUST have `isStreaming: true`.\n230:- Deltas for entries with role `user` or `tool` are invalid and SHOULD be ignored.\n232:### 5.4 entry_commit\n250:- The client MUST set `isStreaming` to `false` on the entry at the given index.\n251:- After a commit, the entry's content is considered complete and stable.\n253:### 5.5 entry_skip\n275:#### Limits of retraction\n283:- The client MUST track skipped indices to adjust subsequent `entry_upsert`, `entry_delta`, and `entry_commit` indices. Server indices diverge from the client array when entries are skipped.\n284:- The adjustment formula: for any incoming index, subtract the count of skipped indices that are less than or equal to it. This maps the server index to the correct client array position.\n285:- A retracted entry is NOT a skipped entry and MUST NOT be counted in that adjustment: it still occupies its position in the client array.\n286:- Skipped indices are always monotonically increasing within a session.\n287:- The client MUST reset its tracked skipped indices on disconnect/reconnect.\n289:### 5.6 error\n322:### 5.7 pong\n338:### 5.8 stream_end\n354:- The server MUST send `stream_end` after every `user_message` it receives, including streams where output was suppressed (see [tool-responses.md](../hal-engine-tool-responses/spec.md)), streams that failed after an `error` frame, and a `user_message` that failed validation ([single-terminal-frame](../hal-engine-single-terminal-frame/spec.md)). The one exception is a turn whose socket closes first: it is abandoned, and nothing more is sent ([abandon-on-close](../hal-engine-abandon-on-close/spec.md)).\n355:- `stream_end` follows every stream even after an error: a failed run sends its `error` frame first and `stream_end` last.\n356:- An `error` frame does not by itself end a stream. It is advisory: it may concern the run in flight or an unrelated frame, such as a malformed `ping`.\n357:- The client SHOULD use this message to clear any \"processing\" or \"loading\" indicators.\n358:- The client MUST treat a run as complete only at `stream_end` or when the socket closes. Unparseable JSON is the one frame answered with `INVALID_FORMAT` and no `stream_end`, because the server cannot tell what it was meant to be; for it the client falls back on the socket closing.\n360:## 6. SessionEntry Objects\n364:### 6.1 UserEntry\n382:### 6.2 AssistantEntry\n403:### 6.3 ThinkingEntry\n424:### 6.4 ToolEntry\n449:## 7. Entry Lifecycle\n453:### 7.1 Streaming Entries (assistant, thinking)\n468:### 7.2 Non-Streaming Entries (user, tool)\n479:## 8. Conversation Sequence\n495:### 8.1 Example: Full Conversation Exchange\n518:### 8.2 Example: Tool with Suppression and Index Skip\n546:### 8.3 Example: Suppression After the Model Has Already Spoken\n566:### 8.4 Example: Error During Streaming\n582:## 9. Tool Execution Loop\n604:## 10. Heartbeat\n608:### 10.1 WebSocket-Level Ping (Server-Initiated)\n612:### 10.2 Application-Level Ping (Client-Initiated)\n616:## 11. Connection Termination\n618:### 11.1 Client Disconnect\n622:### 11.2 Server Shutdown\n626:### 11.3 Reconnection\n630:- Base delay: 1,000 ms\n631:- Formula: `min(1000 * 2^retryCount + jitter, 31000)` where jitter is 0-1000 ms random\n632:- Maximum retries: 5\n633:- On reconnection success, any queued `user_message` messages MUST be flushed immediately.\n634:- On reconnection, the client MUST clear its local entries array and SHOULD send `?sessionId=` with the id from its last `connected` frame. When the server has resume enabled and the conversation is the user's own, it answers `resumed: true` and replays the conversation; otherwise the client is starting a new session.\n640:## 12. Security Considerations\n642:- Access tokens are transmitted in the `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` request header, which keeps them out of the request line and so out of a URL-based access log. They are not hidden from logging in general: an ingress or proxy that captures request headers records them, as it records an `Authorization` header, so header capture must redact `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` too.\n643:- The server answers with the `hal.v1` marker, so the token is never written into the 101 response headers or held as the connected socket's `protocol` property. An offer without the marker is answered with no subprotocol, so no offered value is ever echoed.\n644:- No offered subprotocol value is written to the engine's own log at any level. Every place a credential exists during a connection - request headers, the socket's and the session's `authHeaders`, and the tool context - is listed in [the subprotocol marker spec](../hal-engine-subprotocol-marker/spec.md#where-a-credential-exists).\n645:- Message content is validated at the server boundary. Content exceeding 10,000 characters is rejected.\n646:- The server validates all incoming messages against known types. Unrecognized types receive an `INVALID_MESSAGE` error.\n648:## 13. Connection Termination\n618:### 11.1 Client Disconnect\n622:### 11.2 Server Shutdown\n626:### 11.3 Reconnection\n630:- Base delay: 1,000 ms\n631:- Formula: `min(1000 * 2^retryCount + jitter, 31000)` where jitter is 0-1000 ms random\n632:- Maximum retries: 5\n633:- On reconnection success, any queued `user_message` messages MUST be flushed immediately.\n634:- On reconnection, the client MUST clear its local entries array and SHOULD send `?sessionId=` with the id from its last `connected` frame. When the server has resume enabled and the conversation is the user's own, it answers `resumed: true` and replays the conversation; otherwise the client is starting a new session.\n640:## 12. Security Considerations\n642:- Access tokens are transmitted in the `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` request header, which keeps them out of the request line and so out of a URL-based access log. They are not hidden from logging in general: an ingress or proxy that captures request headers records them, as it records an `Authorization` header, so header capture must redact `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` too.\n643:- The server answers with the `hal.v1` marker, so the token is never written into the 101 response headers or held as the connected socket's `protocol` property. An offer without the marker is answered with no subprotocol, so no offered value is ever echoed.\n644:- No offered subprotocol value is written to the engine's own log at any level. Every place a credential exists during a connection - request headers, the socket's and the session's `authHeaders`, and the tool context - is listed in [the subprotocol marker spec](../hal-engine-subprotocol-marker/spec.md#where-a-credential-exists).\n645:- Message content is validated at the server boundary. Content exceeding 10,000 characters is rejected.\n646:- The server validates all incoming messages against known types. Unrecognized types receive an `INVALID_MESSAGE` error.\n648:## 13. Source Files\n\n1:# WebSocket Protocol Specification\n17:## 1. Introduction\n23:### 1.1 Terminology\n27:### 1.2 Transport\n31:## 2. Server-to-Client Messages\n165:### 5.1 connected\n179:### 5.2 entry_upsert\n208:### 5.3 entry_delta\n232:### 5.4 entry_commit\n253:### 5.5 entry_skip\n275:#### Limits of retraction\n289:### 5.6 error\n322:### 5.7 pong\n338:### 5.8 stream_end\n360:## 6. type {IncomingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\n\nconst MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH = 10000;\n\ninterface ValidationSuccess {\n  valid: true;\n  data: IncomingMessage;\n}\n\ninterface ValidationFailure {\n  valid: false;\n  error: string;\n}\n\nexport type ValidationResult = ValidationSuccess | ValidationFailure;\n\nexport function validateMessage(payload: unknown): ValidationResult {\n  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must be an object'};\n  }\n\n  const message = payload as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n  if (typeof message.type !== 'string') {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must have a type field'};\n  }\n\n  switch (message.type) {\n    case 'user_message':\n      return validateUserMessage(message);\n    case 'ping':\n      return validatePingMessage(message);\n    default:\n      return {valid: false, error: `Unknown message type: ${message.type}`};\n  }\n}\n\nfunction validateUserMessage(message: Record<string, unknown>): ValidationResult {\n  if (typeof message.content !== 'string') {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'user_message must have content string'};\n  }\n\n  const trimmed = message.content.trim();\n\n  if (trimmed.length === 0) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message content cannot be empty'};\n  }\n\n  if (message.content.length > MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH) {\n    return {valid: false, error: `Message content too long (max ${MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH} chars)`};\n  }\n\n  return {\n    valid: true,\n    data: {\n      type: 'user_message',\n      content: message.content,\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction validatePingMessage(message: Record<string, unknown>): ValidationResult {\n  if (typeof message.timestamp !== 'number') {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'ping must have timestamp number'};\n  }\n\n  return {\n    valid: true,\n    data: {\n      type: 'ping',\n      timestamp: message.timestamp,\n    },\n  };\n}\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {SessionEntry} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport {ErrorCodes} from '../../types/messages.js';\nimport type {OutgoingMessage} from state.suppressOutput, entries: session.entries.length});\n}\n\n// Diagnostics only: counts a run of text chunks so it is logged once, not per token.\nfunction logChunk(chunk: MessageChunk, if (chunk.type === 'tool_result') {\n    log.debug('stream', 'tool_result chunk', {\n      messageCount: chunk.clientMessages.length,\n      types: chunk.clientMessages.map(m => m.type),\n    });\n    return 0;\n  }\n\n  log.debug('stream', 'chunk', {type: chunk.type});\n  return 0;\n}\n\nfunction processChunk(\n  ws: WebSocket,\n  session: ChatSession,\n  state: StreamState,\n  parser: processSuppressChunk(ws, session, state);\n  if (chunk.type === 'stop') return processStopChunk(ws, session, state, parser);\n}\n\nfunction processTextChunk(\n  ws: WebSocket,\n  session: ChatSession,\n  state: StreamState,\n  parser: ThinkingTagParser,\n  text: string\n): void {\n  const segments = parser.push(text);\n  for (const segment of segments) {\n    handleTextSegment(ws, session, state, segment);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction handleTextSegment(ws: WebSocket, session: ChatSession, state: StreamState, segment: ParsedSegment): void {\n  if (segment.type === 'thinking') {\n http.Server;\n  wss: WebSocketServer;\n  start: (port?: number) => Promise<void>;\n  stop: () => Promise<void>;\n}\n\nexport function createServer(options: HalServerOptions): HalServer {\n  const [];\n\n  const deps = {\n    wsAuth: options.wsAuth,\n    sessionStore: options.sessionStore,\n    handleMessage,\n    basePath,\n    onConnect: options.onConnect,\n    onDisconnect: options.onDisconnect,\n    resume: options.resume,\n  };\n\n  const handleUpgrade = createUpgradeHandler(wss, deps);\n  const handleConnection = createConnectionHandler(deps, examplePrompts);\n\n  const server = http.createServer(options.app);\n\n  server.on('upgrade', (request, socket, head) => {\n    handleUpgrade(request, socket, head);\n  });\n\n  wss.on('connection', ws => {\n    handleConnection(ws as ExtWebSocket);\n  });\n\n  const heartbeatMs = options.heartbeatIntervalMs ?? 30_000;\n\n  const heartbeatInterval = setInterval(() => {\n    wss.clients.forEach(ws => {\n      const extWs = ws as ExtWebSocket;\n      if (!extWs.isAlive) {\n        extWs.terminate();\n        return;\n      }\n      extWs.isAlive = false;\n      extWs.ping();\n    });\n  }, heartbeatMs);\n\n  // The listening socket should hold the process open, not the heartbeat: an engine never started must not.\n  heartbeatInterval.unref();\n\n  wss.on('close', () => clearInterval(heartbeatInterval));\n\n  return {\n    server,\n    wss,\n    start: (port?: number) =>\n      new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {\n        // Refused before the handlers are touched: `listen` throws on a running server, and the swap below stripped its handler.\n        if (server.listening) {\n          reject(new Error('HAL Engine is already started'));\n          return;\n        }\n\n        // ?? not ||, so a configured port 0 means \"let the OS choose\" rather than 8086.\n        const p = port ?? options.port ?? {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Mcy3jk8i46sezxZjiAJrS3","type":"tool_result","content":"import type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {Express} from 'express';\nimport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport type {HalServer, HalServerOptions} from './createServer.js';\nimport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\nimport type {ChatOrchestrator} from '../orchestration/chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport type {MessageChunk} from '../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../types/session.js';\nimport type {SessionStore} from '../types/sessionStore.js';\n\n// Shared by the suites that need a real upgrade over a real socket, which no double can stand in for.\n\nconst servers: Array<{stop: () => Promise<void>}> = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n\n/** An orchestrator that answers once and stops, which is what commits the turn's assistant entry. */\nexport const answeringOrchestrator: ChatOrchestrator = {\n  processMessage: async () => 'hi',\n  async *processMessageStream(): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n    yield {type: 'text', text: 'hi'};\n    yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n  },\n};\n\n/** Starts an engine on an ephemeral port and returns the URL a client should open. */\nexport async function startEngine(\n  sessionStore: SessionStore,\n  onDisconnect?: (sessionId: string) => void\n): Promise<string> {\n  const {url} = await startEngineWith({sessionStore, onDisconnect});\n  return url;\n}\n\n/** Starts an engine with whatever a suite overrides, returning the server itself beside the URL a client should open. */\nexport async function startEngineWith(\n  options: Partial<HalServerOptions> & Pick<HalServerOptions, 'sessionStore'>\n): Promise<{url: string; hal: HalServer}> {\n  const hal = createServer({\n    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    wsAuth: async () => ({id: 'u1'}),\n    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n    ...options,\n  });\n  await hal.start(0);\n  servers.push(hal);\n  const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n  return {url: `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws/c1`, hal};\n}\n\n/** Opens a client that the suite's teardown will close, whether its test passed or failed. */\nexport function connectClient(url: string, token = 't'): WebSocket {\n  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, token]);\n  clients.push(client);\n  return client;\n}\n\n/** A promise a test settles by hand, to hold a stream or a hook at a point of its choosing. */\nexport function deferred(): {until: Promise<void>; release: () => void} {\n  let release = (): void => undefined;\n  const until = new Promise<void>(resolve => {\n    release = resolve;\n  });\n  return {until, release};\n}\n\n/** Resolves with every frame type seen up to and including the first `until` frame. */\nexport function framesUntil(client: WebSocket, until: string): Promise<string[]> {\n  const seen: string[] = [];\n  return new Promise<string[]>(resolve => {\n    client.on('message', raw => {\n      const {type} = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as {type: string};\n      seen.push(type);\n      if (type === until) resolve(seen);\n    });\n  });\n}\n\n/** Opens a client that asks one question the moment the socket opens, resolving on the first `until` frame. */\nexport function askOnOpen(\n  url: string,\n  {until = 'stream_end', token}: {until?: string; token?: string} = {}\n): {client: WebSocket; finished: Promise<string[]>} {\n  const client = connectClient(url, token);\n  const finished = framesUntil(client, until);\n  client.on('open', () => client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'})));\n  return {client, finished};\n}\n\n/** The session id the client's `connected` frame announces, read after the fact; attach it before any frame arrives. */\nexport function announcedId(client: WebSocket): () => string {\n  let sessionId = '';\n  client.on('message', raw => {\n    const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as {type: string; sessionId?: string};\n    if (frame.type === 'connected') sessionId = frame.sessionId ?? '';\n  });\n  return () => sessionId;\n}\n\n/** Tears every socket and server down, so a red test reports instead of hanging the run. */\nexport async function stopEngines(): Promise<void> {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) await hal.stop();\n}\n\n/** The one session a store holds, for asserting what survived a close. */\nexport async function onlySession(store: SessionStore, sessionId: string): Promise<ChatSession | undefined> {\n  return (await store.get(sessionId)) as ChatSession | undefined;\n}\n----\nimport {once} from 'node:events';\nimport type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {Express} from 'express';\nimport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\nimport type {SessionResumeOptions} from './ws/sessionResume.js';\nimport {answeringOrchestrator} from './wsTestSupport.js';\nimport '../types/sessionStore.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../shared/logger.js';\n\n// Real upgrades over a real socket: what a reconnect is sent is the contract, and no double can observe it.\n\ninterface Frame {\n  type: string;\n  sessionId?: string;\n  resumed?: boolean;\n  entryCount?: number;\n  resumeFailure?: string;\n  index?: number;\n  entry?: SessionEntry;\n}\n\nconst NEVER_ISSUED = '0f1e2d3c-4b5a-4968-8776-655443322110';\nconst RESUME_ON: SessionResumeOptions = {enabled: true};\nconst LATEST_ON: SessionResumeOptions = {enabled: true, latest: true};\nconst HALF_ANSWER: SessionEntry = {\n  role: 'assistant',\n  content: 'half',\n  timestamp: '2026-09-30T08:00:01.000Z',\n  isStreaming: true,\n};\n\n// A tool's suppression lands while the answer is still open, as when a provider ends a tool round with no stop.\nconst suppressingMidAnswer: ChatOrchestrator = {\n  processMessage: async () => 'before after',\n  async *processMessageStream(): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n    yield {type: 'text', text: 'before'};\n    yield {type: 'suppress_output'} as unknown as MessageChunk;\n    yield {type: 'text', text: ' after'};\n    yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n  },\n};\n\nconst servers: Array<ReturnType<typeof createServer>> = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n\nconst eventually = async (check: () => boolean): Promise<void> => {\n  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 50 && !check(); attempt++) await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));\n};\n\n// Silenced for the whole file: the server logs every connection and close, and these tests read frames, not logs.\nbeforeAll(() => {\n  setLogger({debug: () => undefined, info: () => undefined, warn: () => undefined, error: () => {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015r6R8guFry1Z7XbxbzwJBa","type":"tool_result","content":"import type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport type {\n  SessionStore,\n  SessionCreateOptions,\n  SessionListOptions,\n  SessionLookup,\n  SessionSummary,\n} from '../../types/sessionStore.js';\nimport {cappedLimit, cursorAt, newestFirst, sitsAfter} from './sessionListWindow.js';\n\n// Eight hours: a memory bound, not a retention decision (specs/hal-engine-session-lifetime/spec.md).\nconst DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS = 8 * 60 * 60 * 1000;\n\nexport interface InMemorySessionStoreOptions {\n  maxAgeMs?: number;\n}\n\n// The creation time is held beside the session rather than on it, so it never reaches the public ChatSession.\ninterface HeldSession {\n  session: ChatSession;\n  createdAt: number;\n}\n\nexport class InMemorySessionStore implements SessionStore {\n  private readonly sessions = new Map<string, HeldSession>();\n  private readonly maxAgeMs: number;\n\n  constructor(options: InMemorySessionStoreOptions = {}) {\n    this.maxAgeMs = options.maxAgeMs ?? DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS;\n  }\n\n  create(sessionId: string, userId: string | number, options?: SessionCreateOptions): ChatSession {\n    this.sweepExpired();\n\n    const session: ChatSession = {\n      sessionId,\n      userId,\n      entries: [],\n      authHeaders: options?.authHeaders,\n      workspaceId: options?.workspaceId,\n    };\n    this.sessions.set(sessionId, {session, createdAt: Date.now()});\n    return session;\n  }\n\n  get(sessionId: string): ChatSession | undefined {\n    const held = this.sessions.get(sessionId);\n    if (held === undefined) return undefined;\n    if (!this.hasExpired(held)) return held.session;\n\n    this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  /** One shot: a session past `maxAgeMs` answers `expired` once and is evicted, never kept as a tombstone. */\n  lookup(sessionId: string): SessionLookup {\n    const held = this.sessions.get(sessionId);\n    if (held === undefined) return {status: 'missing'};\n    if (!this.hasExpired(held)) return {status: 'active', session: held.session};\n\n    this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n    return {status: 'expired', userId: held.session.userId};\n  }\n\n  delete(sessionId: string): boolean {\n    return this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n  }\n\n  // Eviction and erasure coincide for a store that is only a cache; they do not for a durable one.\n  evict(sessionId: string): boolean {\n    return this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n  }\n\n  count(): number {\n    return this.sessions.size;\n  }\n\n  clear(): void {\n    this.sessions.clear();\n  }\n(eval):1: no matches found: --include=*.ts","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"408fc1a6-1225-4e31-876a-60790501971d","uuid":"6c4d6408-0ff2-4469-b936-616da1f308e5","timestamp":"2026-11-02T10:40:04.666Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"import type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport type {\n  SessionStore,\n  SessionCreateOptions,\n  SessionListOptions,\n  SessionLookup,\n  SessionSummary,\n} from '../../types/sessionStore.js';\nimport {cappedLimit, cursorAt, newestFirst, sitsAfter} from './sessionListWindow.js';\n\n// Eight hours: a memory bound, not a retention decision (specs/hal-engine-session-lifetime/spec.md).\nconst DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS = 8 * 60 * 60 * 1000;\n\nexport interface InMemorySessionStoreOptions {\n  maxAgeMs?: number;\n}\n\n// The creation time is held beside the session rather than on it, so it never reaches the public ChatSession.\ninterface HeldSession {\n  session: ChatSession;\n  createdAt: number;\n}\n\nexport class InMemorySessionStore implements SessionStore {\n  private readonly sessions = new Map<string, HeldSession>();\n  private readonly maxAgeMs: number;\n\n  constructor(options: InMemorySessionStoreOptions = {}) {\n    this.maxAgeMs = options.maxAgeMs ?? DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS;\n  }\n\n  create(sessionId: string, userId: string | number, options?: SessionCreateOptions): ChatSession {\n    this.sweepExpired();\n\n    const session: ChatSession = {\n      sessionId,\n      userId,\n      entries: [],\n      authHeaders: options?.authHeaders,\n      workspaceId: options?.workspaceId,\n    };\n    this.sessions.set(sessionId, {session, createdAt: Date.now()});\n    return session;\n  }\n\n  get(sessionId: string): ChatSession | undefined {\n    const held = this.sessions.get(sessionId);\n    if (held === undefined) return undefined;\n    if (!this.hasExpired(held)) return held.session;\n\n    this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  /** One shot: a session past `maxAgeMs` answers `expired` once and is evicted, never kept as a tombstone. */\n  lookup(sessionId: string): SessionLookup {\n    const held = this.sessions.get(sessionId);\n    if (held === undefined) return {status: 'missing'};\n    if (!this.hasExpired(held)) return {status: 'active', session: held.session};\n\n    this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n    return {status: 'expired', userId: held.session.userId};\n  }\n\n  delete(sessionId: string): boolean {\n    return this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n  }\n\n  // Eviction and erasure coincide for a store that is only a cache; they do not for a durable one.\n  evict(sessionId: string): boolean {\n    return this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n  }\n\n  count(): number {\n    return this.sessions.size;\n  }\n\n  clear(): void {\n    this.sessions.clear();\n  }\n(eval):1: no matches found: --include=*.ts","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_015r6R8guFry1Z7XbxbzwJBa","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011CgdxcizUFui2wimVQR2e1","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_019jZgGLMr4eBUeERtXMCrEu","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/efdf7de3fa0c/workspace/target/src/transport/listConversations.acceptance.test.ts","content":"import {jest} from '@jest/globals';\nimport {once} from 'node:events';\nimport type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {Express} from 'express';\nimport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport type {HalServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\nimport {answeringOrchestrator} from './wsTestSupport.js';\nimport {InMemorySessionStore} from '../infrastructure/stores/inMemorySessionStore.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../shared/logger.js';\n\n// Real upgrades over a real socket: the list a client is sent over the wire is the contract, and no double can observe it.\n\ninterface Frame {\n  type: string;\n  sessionId?: string;\n  code?: string;\n  conversations?: Array<{sessionId: string; createdAt: string; updatedAt: string; entryCount: number}>;\n}\n\nconst USER = 'u1';\nconst OTHER_USER = 'u2';\n\nconst servers: HalServer[] = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n\n// Silenced for the whole file: the server logs every connection, and these tests read frames, not logs.\nbeforeAll(() => {\n  setLogger({debug: () => undefined, info: () => undefined, warn: () => undefined, error: () => undefined});\n});\n\nafterAll(() => {\n  setLogger();\n});\n\nafterEach(async () => {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) await hal.stop();\n});\n\n// The authenticated user is the one the upgrade names; the store is real, seeded before any socket opens.\nconst startEngine = async (store: InMemorySessionStore, history?: {enabled: boolean}) => {\n  const hal = createServer({\n    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    wsAuth: async () => ({id: USER}),\n    sessionStore: store,\n    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n    history,\n  });\n  await hal.start(0);\n  servers.push(hal);\n  const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n  return `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws/c1`;\n};\n\n// Opens a client, sends one frame once `connected` arrives, and resolves with the frames that answer it.\nconst askFor = (url: string, question: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<Frame[]> => {\n  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, 't']);\n  clients.push(client);\n  return new Promise<Frame[]>(resolve => {\n    const frames: Frame[] = [];\n    client.on('message', raw => {\n      const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as Frame;\n      frames.push(frame);\n      if (frame.type === 'connected') client.send(JSON.stringify(question));\n      if (frame.type === 'conversation_list' || frame.type === 'error') resolve(frames.slice(1));\n    });\n  });\n};\n\n// Seeds a conversation whose last activity is `at`, so the order a list answers in is the order the test sets.\nconst seed = (store: InMemorySessionStore, sessionId: string, userId: string, at: string): void => {\n  const session = store.create(sessionId, userId);\n  session.entries.push({role: 'user', content: 'hello', timestamp: at});\n};\n\ndescribe('list_conversations over the WebSocket', () => {\n  it('answers the connecting user’s conversations as one conversation_list, newest first, with ISO times', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    seed(store, 'conv-oldest', USER, '2026-01-01T10:00:00.000Z');\n    seed(store, 'conv-middle', USER, '2026-01-02T10:00:00.000Z');\n    seed(store, 'conv-newest', USER, '2026-01-03T10:00:00.000Z');\n    const url = await startEngine(store, {enabled: true});\n\n    const frames = await askFor(url, {type: 'list_conversations'});\n\n    expect(frames.map(frame => frame.type)).toEqual(['conversation_list']);\n    const rows = frames[0].conversations ?? [];\n    const seeded = rows.map(row => row.sessionId).filter(id => id.startsWith('conv-'));\n    expect(seeded).toEqual(['conv-newest', 'conv-middle', 'conv-oldest']);\n    for (const row of rows) {\n      expect(new Date(row.createdAt).toISOString()).toBe(row.createdAt);\n      expect(new Date(row.updatedAt).toISOString()).toBe(row.updatedAt);\n    }\n  });\n\n  it('ignores a userId in the frame and lists by the connection’s authenticated user', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    seed(store, 'conv-mine', USER, '2026-01-01T10:00:00.000Z');\n    seed(store, 'conv-theirs', OTHER_USER, '2026-01-02T10:00:00.000Z');\n    const listFor = jest.spyOn(store, 'listFor');\n    const url = await startEngine(store, {enabled: true});\n\n    const frames = await askFor(url, {type: 'list_conversations', userId: OTHER_USER});\n\n    expect(frames.map(frame => frame.type)).toEqual(['conversation_list']);\n    const ids = (frames[0].conversations ?? []).map(row => row.sessionId);\n    expect(ids).toContain('conv-mine');\n    expect(ids).not.toContain('conv-theirs');\n    expect(listFor.mock.calls[0]?.[0]).toBe(USER);\n  });\n\n  it('answers UNSUPPORTED with no conversation_list when transport.history is absent', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    seed(store, 'conv-mine', USER, '2026-01-01T10:00:00.000Z');\n    const url = await startEngine(store);\n\n    const frames = await askFor(url, {type: 'list_conversations'});\n\n    expect(frames.map(frame => frame.type)).toEqual(['error']);\n    expect(frames[0].code).toBe('UNSUPPORTED');\n  });\n});\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2210,"cache_read_input_tokens":69010,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2210},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"408fc1a6-1225-4e31-876a-60790501971d","uuid":"673d0c45-5287-4672-9036-be568393a8a7","timestamp":"2026-11-02T10:40:44.597Z","request_id":"req_011Cgdxcgww4vspTNF7kmpmn"}

stops, so no close is logged after the logger above is restored.\nafterEach(async () => {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {\n    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 50 && hal.wss.clients.size > 0; attempt++) {\n      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));\n    }\n    await hal.stop();\n  }\n});","originalFile":"import {jest} from '@jest/globals';\nimport {once} from 'node:events';\nimport type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {Express} from 'express';\nimport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport type {HalServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\nimport {answeringOrchestrator} from './wsTestSupport.js';\nimport {InMemorySessionStore} from '../infrastructure/stores/inMemorySessionStore.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../shared/logger.js';\n\n// Real upgrades over a real socket: the list a client is sent over the wire is the contract, and no double can observe it.\n\ninterface Frame {\n  type: string;\n  sessionId?: string;\n  code?: string;\n  conversations?: Array<{sessionId: string; createdAt: string; updatedAt: string; entryCount: number}>;\n}\n\nconst USER = 'u1';\nconst OTHER_USER = 'u2';\n\nconst servers: HalServer[] = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n\n// Silenced for the whole file: the server logs every connection, and these tests read frames, not logs.\nbeforeAll(() => {\n  setLogger({debug: () => undefined, info: () => undefined, warn: () => undefined, error: () => undefined});\n});\n\nafterAll(() => {\n  setLogger();\n});\n\nafterEach(async () => {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) await hal.stop();\n});\n\n// The authenticated user is the one the upgrade names; the store is real, seeded before any socket opens.\nconst startEngine = async (store: InMemorySessionStore, history?: {enabled: boolean}) => {\n  const hal = createServer({\n    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    wsAuth: async () => ({id: USER}),\n    sessionStore: store,\n    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n    history,\n  });\n  await hal.start(0);\n  servers.push(hal);\n  const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n  return `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws/c1`;\n};\n\n// Opens a client, sends one frame once `connected` arrives, and resolves with the frames that answer it.\nconst askFor = (url: string, question: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<Frame[]> => {\n  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, 't']);\n  clients.push(client);\n  return new Promise<Frame[]>(resolve => {\n    const frames: Frame[] = [];\n    client.on('message', raw => {\n      const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as Frame;\n      frames.push(frame);\n      if (frame.type === 'connected') client.send(JSON.stringify(question));\n      if (frame.type === 'conversation_list' || frame.type === 'error') resolve(frames.slice(1));\n    });\n  });\n};\n\n// Seeds a conversation whose last activity is `at`, so the order a list answers in is the order the test sets.\nconst seed = (store: InMemorySessionStore, sessionId: string, userId: string, at: string): void => {\n  const session = store.create(sessionId, userId);\n  session.entries.push({role: 'user', content: 'hello', timestamp: at});\n};\n\ndescribe('list_conversations over the WebSocket', () => {\n  it('answers the connecting user’s conversations as one conversation_list, newest first, with ISO times', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    seed(store, 'conv-oldest', USER, '2026-01-01T10:00:00.000Z');\n    seed(store, 'conv-middle', USER, '2026-01-02T10:00:00.000Z');\n    seed(store, 'conv-newest', USER, '2026-01-03T10:00:00.000Z');\n    const url = await startEngine(store, {enabled: true});\n\n    const frames = await askFor(url, {type: 'list_conversations'});\n\n    expect(frames.map(frame => frame.type)).toEqual(['conversation_list']);\n    const rows = frames[0].conversations ?? [];\n    const seeded = rows.map(row => row.sessionId).filter(id => id.startsWith('conv-'));\n    expect(seeded).toEqual(['conv-newest', 'conv-middle', 'conv-oldest']);\n    for (const row of rows) {\n      expect(new Date(row.createdAt).toISOString()).toBe(row.createdAt);\n      expect(new Date(row.updatedAt).toISOString()).toBe(row.updatedAt);\n    }\n  });\n\n  it('ignores a userId in the frame and lists by the connection’s authenticated user', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    seed(store, 'conv-mine', USER, '2026-01-01T10:00:00.000Z');\n    seed(store, 'conv-theirs', OTHER_USER, '2026-01-02T10:00:00.000Z');\n    const listFor = jest.spyOn(store, 'listFor');\n    const url = await startEngine(store, {enabled: true});\n\n    const frames = await askFor(url, {type: 'list_conversations', userId: OTHER_USER});\n\n    expect(frames.map(frame => frame.type)).toEqual(['conversation_list']);\n    const ids = (frames[0].conversations ?? []).map(row => row.sessionId);\n    expect(ids).toContain('conv-mine');\n    expect(ids).not.toContain('conv-theirs');\n    expect(listFor.mock.calls[0]?.[0]).toBe(USER);\n  });\n\n  it('answers UNSUPPORTED with no conversation_list when transport.history is absent', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    seed(store, 'conv-mine', USER, '2026-01-01T10:00:00.000Z');\n    const url = await startEngine(store);\n\n    const frames = await askFor(url, {type: 'list_conversations'});\n\n    expect(frames.map(frame => frame.type)).toEqual(['error']);\n    expect(frames[0].code).toBe('UNSUPPORTED');\n  });\n});\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":34,"oldLines":9,"newStart":34,"newLines":15,"lines":["   setLogger();"," });"," ","+// Each server sees its sockets close before it stops, so no close is logged after the logger above is restored."," afterEach(async () => {","   for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();","-  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) await hal.stop();","+  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {","+    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 50 && hal.wss.clients.size > 0; attempt++) {","+      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));","+    }","+    await hal.stop();","+  }"," });"," "," // The authenticated user is the one the upgrade names; the store is real, seeded before any socket opens."]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01EZqoqd7pkt6rzraZeqNCnS","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
